(\varphi^{(\infty)},p^{(\infty)})\colon(B_{1}^{(\infty)},{\mathrm{D}}^{(\infty)}_{1})\to(B_{2}^{(\infty)},{\mathrm{D}}^{(\infty)}_{2})
of profinite Lie algebroids.

Notice that if
(B,p_{1},{\mathrm{D}}_{1})
is a prolongation of a relative algebroid
(B,{\overline{\nabla}},{\mathrm{D}})
, then the torsion class of
(B,p_{1},{\mathrm{D}}_{1})
is in the kernel of
(p_{1})_{*}
. Hence, the proposition implies:

Corollary 3.22
.

If a relative algebroid
(B,{\overline{\nabla}},{\mathrm{D}})
admits a prolongation
(B,p_{1},{\mathrm{D}}_{1})
, then its torsion class must vanish.

Proof.

First, we show that
\varphi_{*}
and
p_{*}
intertwine the tableau maps. For that, notice that if
\alpha\in\Omega_{(B_{1},{\overline{\nabla}}^{1})}
then, for any
X\in\mathcal{F}
, we have

({\overline{\nabla}}^{1}_{X}{\mathrm{D}}_{1})\alpha={\overline{\nabla}}^{1}_{X}({\mathrm{D}}_{1}\alpha).

Using this and the definition of the tableaux, we find for
\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}_{(B_{2},{\overline{\nabla}}^{2})}
:

\displaystyle(\varphi_{*}\tau_{1}(X))\alpha

\displaystyle=\left(\varphi_{*}\left({\overline{\nabla}}^{1}_{X}{\mathrm{D}}_{1}\right)\right)\alpha=\varphi_{*}\left({\overline{\nabla}}^{1}_{X}\left({\mathrm{D}}_{1}\varphi^{*}\alpha\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{*}\left({\overline{\nabla}}^{1}_{X}\varphi^{*}({\mathrm{D}}_{2}\alpha)\right)=\varphi_{*}\left(\varphi^{*}{\overline{\nabla}}^{2}_{p_{*}(X)}({\mathrm{D}}_{2}\alpha)\right)

\displaystyle={\overline{\nabla}}^{2}_{p_{*}(X)}({\mathrm{D}}_{2}\alpha)=({\overline{\nabla}}^{2}_{p_{*}(X)}{\mathrm{D}}_{2})(\alpha)=\tau_{2}(p_{*}(X))(\alpha).

Since
\varphi_{*}
intertwines the Spencer differentials, it induces maps on the Spencer complexes of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
, commuting with the differentials, and therefore
\varphi_{*}
descends to the level of cohomology. To check that it relates the torsion classes, let
\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1}
be a pointwise lift of
{\mathrm{D}}_{1}
above
x\in M_{1}
. Its torsion is the 2-derivation
T^{1}\big|_{\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1}}=\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1}\circ{\mathrm{D}}_{1}
. Because
{\mathrm{D}}_{1}
and
{\mathrm{D}}_{2}
are
\varphi
-related, the derivation
\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{2}:=\varphi_{*}(\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1})
is a pointwise lift of
{\mathrm{D}}_{2}
above
p(x)\in M_{2}
. The 2-derivations
T^{1}\big|_{\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1}}
and
T^{2}\big|_{\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{2}}
are also
\varphi_{*}
-related since

\displaystyle\varphi_{*}\left(T^{1}\big|_{\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1}}\right)(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{*}(\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1}\circ{\mathrm{D}}_{1})(\alpha)=\varphi_{*}\circ\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1}\circ{\mathrm{D}}_{1}\circ\varphi^{*}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\varphi_{*}\circ\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1}\circ\varphi^{*}\circ{\mathrm{D}}_{2}(\alpha)=\varphi_{*}(\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1})\circ{\mathrm{D}}_{2}(\alpha)

\displaystyle=\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{2}\circ{\mathrm{D}}_{2}(\alpha)=T^{2}\big|_{\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{2}}(\alpha),

for any
\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}_{(B_{2},{\overline{\nabla}}^{2})}
. Passing down to the Spencer cohomology, item (i) follows.

Item (ii) follows by observing that, for
\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}_{(B_{2},{\overline{\nabla}}^{2})}
, one has

\displaystyle(\varphi\circ\theta)^{*}{\mathrm{D}}_{2}\alpha

\displaystyle=\theta^{*}\varphi^{*}{\mathrm{D}}_{2}\alpha=\theta^{*}{\mathrm{D}}_{1}\varphi^{*}\alpha={\mathrm{d}}(\varphi\circ\theta)^{*}\alpha.

In order to prove item (iii) it is enough to prove the case
k=1
. By the previous calculation, if
\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1}
is a
torsionless
lift of
{\mathrm{D}}_{1}
, then
\varphi_{*}(\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1})
is also a torsionless lift of
{\mathrm{D}}_{2}
. So
\varphi_{*}
restricts to a map between the base spaces of the first prolongation
p^{(1)}:=\varphi_{*}\colon M_{1}^{(1)}\to M_{2}^{(1)}
, satisfying

(p_{2})_{1}\circ p^{(1)}=(p_{2})_{1}\circ\varphi_{*}=p\circ(p_{1})_{1}.

This map and
\varphi
combine into a map between the pullbacks
B^{(1)}_{i}=(p_{i})_{1}^{*}B_{i}
:

\varphi^{(1)}:=(\varphi,p^{(1)})\colon B^{(1)}_{1}\to B^{(1)}_{2}.

Note that
\varphi^{(1)}
is again a fiberwise isomorphism and also a map of relative algebroids because of the tautological nature of the derivations
{\mathrm{D}}^{(1)}_{1}
and
{\mathrm{D}}_{2}^{(1)}
. Indeed, we find

\displaystyle{\mathrm{D}}^{(1)}_{1}(\varphi^{*}(\alpha))\big|_{\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1}(\varphi^{*}\alpha)=\varphi^{*}\big(\varphi_{*}(\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1})(\alpha)\big)

\displaystyle=\varphi^{*}\left({\mathrm{D}}^{(1)}_{2}(\alpha)\big|_{\varphi_{*}(\tilde{{\mathrm{D}}}_{1})}\right)=(\varphi^{(1)})^{*}\big({\mathrm{D}}^{(1)}_{2}(\alpha)\big)

for any
\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}_{(B_{2},{\overline{\nabla}}^{2})}
, where we used that
p^{(1)}=\varphi_{*}
.

Finally, item (iv) follows from item (iii).
∎

4.
Constructions

To better understand relative algebroids, we will now develop several important constructions involving them.

4.1.
The universal relative algebroid

Let
A\to N
be any vector bundle, and
p_{1}\colon\mathcal{D}^{k}_{A}\to N
its bundle of
k
-derivations. There is a
tautological
k
-derivation

\breve{\mathrm{D}}
relative to the projection
p_{1}\colon\mathcal{D}^{k}_{A}\to N
, which at each point
{\mathrm{D}}_{x}\in\left(\mathcal{D}^{k}_{A}\right)_{x}
is the derivation itself:

\breve{\mathrm{D}}(\alpha)({\mathrm{D}}_{x})={\mathrm{D}}_{x}\alpha\in\wedge^{k+l}A_{x}^{*},\quad\text{for }\alpha\in\Omega^{l}(A).

Definition 4.1
.

The
universal relative Lie algebroid
of a vector bundle
A\to N
is the triple
(A,p_{1},\breve{\mathrm{D}})
, where
p_{1}\colon\mathcal{D}^{1}_{A}\to N
and
\breve{\mathrm{D}}
is the tautological 1-derivation.

The bundle over
\mathcal{D}^{1}_{A}
of the universal relative algebroid will be denoted
\breve{B}:=p_{1}^{*}A
. In order to justify the use of the term “universal”, let us introduce the following notation.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
(A,p,{\mathrm{D}})
be an algebroid relative to a submersion
p\colon M\to N
. The
classifying map

c_{{\mathrm{D}}}
of
(A,p,{\mathrm{D}})
is the composition

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 8.39583pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.39583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.39583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.57945pt\raise 23.00694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 86.1723pt\raise 6.7512pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern 32.39583pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
